Studies

Every run published in this repository, in date order. Each run lists the sub-questions it asked (its goals), where each result is written, and the findings written from it. A goal marked pending has a published result with no finding yet; its headline gives the values as the run's own result file states them. Every file under results/ belongs to exactly one goal or to its run as a whole (runs.json).

2026-10-26 · Core-FP8 expert bytes and FP8 projections checked against K3.25-v1; CuteAFD source audit

Are the Core-FP8 checkpoint's bytes what its provenance says relative to K3.25-v1, and does CuteAFD v3.0.1 share, in source, the vLLM-engine issues D1-D7 and E1 found in earlier evaluation?

check · protocol: none (not a frozen-protocol run) · data: results/2026-10-26-cuteafd-setup-checks

accounting Are the base tensors of the Core-FP8 checkpoint d31ebc92 byte-identical to the local K3.25-v1 0490d2f7 snapshot, as Core-FP8's stitch provenance records?

150,047 of 150,047 base tensors in Core-FP8's stitch provenance hash-equal to the local K3.25-v1 snapshot (140,944,474,104 bytes), with 0 mismatches, 0 missing and 0 local tensors outside the provenance; the other 358 provenance entries come from the FP8 source (179 weights and their block scales).

descriptive For the 179 projection tensors Core-FP8 replaced, how far is the official FP8, dequantized, from K3.25-v1's BF16?

Relative Frobenius norm of (dequantized official FP8 − K3.25-v1 BF16), pooled over the 179 replaced projection tensors: 0.16%; per tensor 0.14% to 0.18%.

accounting Does a plain E4M3 amax/448 128x128 block quantization of K3.25-v1's BF16 reproduce the official FP8 bytes of the 179 tensors Core-FP8 replaced?

Own E4M3 quantization (amax/448, 128x128 blocks) of the BF16 matches 99.99998% of the official FP8 codes (lowest tensor 99.9998%); 37.2% of block scales are bit-equal, and the largest relative scale difference in any tensor is below 2^-23, which the run reads as within one FP32 ulp.

source reading Which CuteAFD v3.0.1 code paths serve 2 RTX GPUs with local experts and no Sparks (attention placement and DSA index cache)?

From source (CuteAFD v3.0.1, not run): on two GPUs the automatic attention placement is `context` (token-split MLA, compact index cache); `heads` (per-token key cache) is chosen only explicitly, and the v3 2-RTX release card reads head split. Both index-cache variants were read; the `layers` placement was not audited separately.

source reading Does CuteAFD v3.0.1 keep all 512 selected index pools (the vLLM-engine issue D1 dropped column 511)?

From source: all 512 selected pools are expanded, plus the open tail pool, into a 2,112-slot row on every path read, and rows of exactly 512 pools take the dense branch; audit verdict: handled correctly. Not audited: whether the b12x top-k kernel's selected set is exact.

source reading Does CuteAFD v3.0.1 have a counterpart of the vLLM-engine issue D2 (index tail ring written without positions)?

From source: there is no tail ring or generic mapping fallback; every slot is addressed from the row's own position and its sequence's pages, and a position outside them is an error. Audit verdict: not applicable (different design).

source reading Are router logits computed in FP32 in CuteAFD v3.0.1 (the vLLM-engine issue D3 computed them in BF16 on SM12x)?

From source: BF16 operands, FP32 accumulation and an FP32 store on both GEMM routes, with bias and selection in FP32; `moe_router_dtype` is not read, so FP32 is unconditional. Audit verdict: handled correctly; no numerical comparison was run.

source reading Does the routed SwiGLU limit reach CuteAFD v3.0.1's compiled expert kernels (the vLLM-engine issue D4 did not pass it)?

From source, for local EXL3 routed experts: the limit is compiled at 10 and checked at startup (a package without it is refused); shared and dense MLPs also clamp at 10. Audit verdict: handled correctly. Not read: the clamp inside b12x mixed_trellis; whether activations reach 10 was not measured.

source reading Can a CuteAFD v3.0.1 workspace referenced by a captured CUDA graph be reallocated (the vLLM-engine issue D5: workspace not locked after capture)?

From source: step workspaces are fixed-capacity, allocated before capture and never replaced, and no resize path was found in glm5_flash/engine.rs or the drafter. Audit verdict: not applicable (different design); not exhaustive: EXL3 shared arenas, peer-exchange buffers and token I/O were not audited.

source reading Can stale warm-up block ids reach CuteAFD v3.0.1's tables (the vLLM-engine issue D6)?

From source: tables are rebuilt from the placement every step, padding and warm-up rows carry -1 slots that the writers skip, and per-sequence state is reset or restored before first use. Audit verdict: not applicable (different design).

source reading Does CuteAFD v3.0.1's index-K prefill writer handle chunk starts that are not pool-aligned (the vLLM-engine issue D7)?

From source, in both index caches: a pool that straddles a chunk start reads the sequence's own stored keys (keys cache) or its own tail (compact cache), never other sequences' rows. Audit verdict: handled correctly.

source reading Do CuteAFD v3.0.1's router weights or logits depend on batch size (the vLLM observation E1)?

From source: the E1 mechanism ((s+b)−b rebuild above 1,024 rows) does not apply, since the weight is read from the stored unbiased score and no branch depends on the row count. Whether logits are bit-identical across the 160/161-row GEMV/GEMM route switch could not be determined from source.

source reading Which of CuteAFD v3.0.1's MLP clamps and related keys are read from the checkpoint's config.json?

From source: routed experts, shared expert and dense MLPs clamp at 10 from compiled constants, none reads `swiglu_limit` from config.json, and no equality check was found, so a checkpoint declaring another value would not be detected (the local K3.25 declares 10.0). `routed_scaling_factor`, `index_topk` and `index_kpool` are read; `moe_router_dtype` is not.

checkdoneREADME.md, bytecheck/report-local-0490d2f7.jsonSC-bytes
FP8For the 179 projection tensors Core-FP8 replaced, how far is the official FP8 from K3.25-v1's BF16, and does a plain E4M3 amax/448 128x128 block quantization of the BF16 reproduce the official FP8 bytes?
Official FP8, dequantized, differs from K3.25-v1's BF16 by 0.16% (relative Frobenius norm, all 179 tensors). Quantizing the BF16 to E4M3 with amax/448 scales over 128x128 blocks reproduces 99.99998% of the official codes, with scales within one FP32 ulp (only 37.2% bit-equal).
checkdoneREADME.md, fp8check/report.jsonSC-fp8-distance, SC-fp8-recipe
PATHSWhich CuteAFD v3.0.1 code paths serve 2 RTX GPUs with local experts and no Sparks (attention placement and DSA index cache)?
From source: `context` (token-split MLA, compact index cache) is the automatic placement on two GPUs; `heads` (per-token key cache) is explicit and is what the v3 2-RTX release card used; both index-cache variants were read, the `layers` placement was not audited separately.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-paths
D1Does CuteAFD v3.0.1 keep all 512 selected index pools (the vLLM-engine issue D1 dropped column 511)?
Handled correctly (from source): all 512 selected pools are expanded, plus the open tail pool, into a 2,112-slot row on every path read; rows of exactly 512 pools take the dense branch. Not audited: whether the b12x top-k kernel's selected set is exact.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d1
D2Does CuteAFD v3.0.1 have a counterpart of the vLLM-engine issue D2 (index tail ring written without positions)?
Not applicable (different design), from source: no tail ring or generic mapping fallback; every slot is addressed from the row's own position and sequence pages, and a position outside them is an error.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d2
D3Are router logits computed in FP32 in CuteAFD v3.0.1 (the vLLM-engine issue D3 computed them in BF16 on SM12x)?
Handled correctly (from source): BF16 operands, FP32 accumulation and FP32 store on both GEMM routes, bias and selection in FP32; `moe_router_dtype` is not read (FP32 is unconditional). No numerical comparison was run.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d3
D4Does the routed SwiGLU limit reach CuteAFD v3.0.1's compiled expert kernels (the vLLM-engine issue D4 did not pass it)?
Handled correctly (from source) for local EXL3 routed experts: compiled at 10 and enforced at startup; shared and dense MLPs also clamp at 10. Not read: the clamp inside b12x mixed_trellis; whether activations reach 10 was not measured.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d4
D5Can a CuteAFD v3.0.1 workspace referenced by a captured CUDA graph be reallocated (the vLLM-engine issue D5: workspace not locked after capture)?
Not applicable (different design), from source: fixed-capacity step workspaces allocated before capture and never replaced; no resize path found in glm5_flash/engine.rs and the drafter. Not exhaustive: EXL3 shared arenas, peer-exchange buffers and token I/O were not audited.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d5
D6Can stale warm-up block ids reach CuteAFD v3.0.1's tables (the vLLM-engine issue D6)?
Not applicable (different design), from source: tables are rebuilt every step, padding and warm-up rows carry -1 slots that writers skip, and per-sequence state is reset or restored before use.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d6
D7Does CuteAFD v3.0.1's index-K prefill writer handle chunk starts that are not pool-aligned (the vLLM-engine issue D7)?
Handled correctly (from source) in both index caches: a straddling pool reads the sequence's own stored keys (keys cache) or its own tail (compact cache), never other sequences' rows.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-d7
E1Do CuteAFD v3.0.1's router weights or logits depend on batch size (the vLLM observation E1)?
The E1 mechanism ((s+b)-b rebuild above 1,024 rows) is not applicable (different design). Whether router logits are bit-identical across the 160/161-row GEMV/GEMM route switch cannot be determined from source; it was measured afterwards in 2026-10-26-e1-router-route.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-e1-mechanism
SWIGLUWhich of CuteAFD v3.0.1's MLP clamps and related keys are read from the checkpoint's config.json?
From source: routed experts, shared expert and dense MLPs clamp at 10 from compiled constants; none read `swiglu_limit` from config.json, and no equality check was found, so a checkpoint declaring another value would not be detected (the local K3.25 declares 10.0). `routed_scaling_factor`, `index_topk` and `index_kpool` are read; `moe_router_dtype` is not.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-swiglu
AUDIT-LIMITSWhat did the source audit not cover?
Source only, no behaviour measured, released image not inspected; submodule read at the pinned commit without verifying the image was built from it; b12x internals, FP8/NVFP4 local expert paths, Spark worker paths and the `layers` placement not read.
source readingdonesource-audit/audit-vllm-items.mdSC-audit-limits

2026-10-26 · CuteAFD v3.0.1 vs tpurtell's vLLM fork v0.9.2: precision, throughput, long output

How do tpurtell's CuteAFD v3.0.1 and his vLLM fork v0.9.2, each as shipped on GLM-5.3-Flash EXL3 K3.25 with identical expert bytes on 2 RTX PRO 6000 Max-Q, compare in teacher-anchored precision, serving throughput and long-output behaviour under a frozen protocol?

measurement · protocol: protocol/PROTOCOL.md · data: results/2026-10-26-cuteafd-vs-vllm

Findings written to the current standard (67)

accounting Do the vLLM fork v0.9.2 and CuteAFD v3.0.1 honour enable_thinking:false with the K3.25 chat template?

The vLLM fork rendered 147 prompt tokens for enable_thinking true and false alike, and with false returned no separate reasoning (0 reasoning characters; the reasoning arrived in the content); the template has no thinking-off switch and ends every prompt with an open think tag, while CuteAFD renders false as an empty think block. The run therefore used thinking on for every T and L request.

descriptive Which long code replies did the terminal-loop detector flag on each engine, and what does the analysis record about them?

The detector flagged code replies on both engines: vLLM fork 6 of 8 at L3 and 1 of 1 at L3s, CuteAFD 2 of 8 at L2 and 4 of 8 at L3; for all 13 the analysis records no exact period and a flag onset before the reasoning ends. The audit's reading that the flagged text is code restated from the reasoning (U5) covers the 7 vLLM replies only.

accounting Did Block T give CuteAFD v3.0.1 and the vLLM fork v0.9.2 identical inputs?

No: 0 of 1,008 paired requests share a nonce first line between the engines (seeds and prompt bodies pair), and the 24 CuteAFD requests at 128K code report 131,096 or 131,095 prompt tokens against 131,072 or 131,071 on vLLM. source reading Besides FP8 projections, FP8 head and KDA FP8, how else do CuteAFD v3.0.1's and the vLLM fork v0.9.2's arithmetic differ, and is F1's KDA arm a single-factor test?

Read from source and logs, not measured: the engines also differ in DSA index-key format (CuteAFD BF16, vLLM FP8), attention placement (token split vs whole layers per GPU), KDA split path, prefill chunk geometry, BF16 logits in vLLM, and CuteAFD's W8A8 prefill GEMMs. F1's KDA arm (GLM5_FLASH_KDA_FP8=off) also removes KDA output sharding, so it changes two things.

descriptive Do the fidelity set's trailing context rows show a reference-side error, or are they sensitive rows?

All 30 trailing context spans are near-copies of one unrelated filler file; on rows 20 or more positions into a span, cross-engine KL is 0.72, 3.14 and 2.53 nats on three windows (0.008-0.020 on the same windows' generated rows), and vLLM against its own repeat is 1.96 nats on one of them (audit). The audit reads these rows as sensitive to any arithmetic change, not as evidence of a reference-side error.

source reading Which launch settings differ between the two engines beyond the protocol's documented deltas?

On the vLLM side, GPU memory utilisation 0.95 instead of the launcher default 0.97, and the v0.9.0 repository's start script run with the v0.9.2 image (whether v0.9.2's own launcher defaults differ was not verified). Between the engines also: drafter policy, attention placement, DSA index-key format, KV record, prefill scheduling, prefix cache, logit precision (BF16 vs F32) and parsing of special tokens in user text.

Q-PBOn panel P-B (tpurtell's cuteafd-fidelity, 0.5K-14.4K), how far is each engine from the BF16 reference on generated rows, paired CuteAFD against vLLM?
Generated rows, CuteAFD v3.0.1 − vLLM fork v0.9.2: against the first vLLM run V1, coarse KL +0.0027 [+0.0007, +0.0046], NLL +0.0168 [+0.0121, +0.0211], top-1 −0.51 pts [−0.85, −0.16]. Corrected after the audit (I1) to pair against the mean of the vLLM runs, because vLLM's multi-chunk prefill is not run-to-run deterministic: against the mean of three vLLM runs over two launches (F3), KL +0.0033 [+0.0016, +0.0049], top-1 −0.46 pts [−0.73, −0.17].

Q-PB-BARDo both engines pass tpurtell's calibrated absolute bar for the P-B set (generated rows, prefill-shaped)?
Both engines pass; CuteAFD v3.0.1 narrowly (0.0484 / 93.33% / 96.31% / 99.48%). For scale, tpurtell's published baseline for the official FP8 model on the same set is KL 0.0216 / top-1 95.77% (quoted, not measured here). The panel's paired bar is read separately in M4.

Q-PB-BUCKETOn P-B generated rows, how does the CuteAFD − vLLM difference vary across the panel's context buckets (0-2K / 2-8K / 8-16K) and blocks?

Q-PB-CTXAre the large KL values on P-B's trailing context rows in tool-call windows reference-side or engine-side?
Context rows show KL ~1.7 nat on both engines (V1 1.69, C1 1.73), concentrated in tool-call windows, and both engines' NLL there (3.61-3.77) is below the reference's own (3.87). After the audit (U4): all 30 trailing spans are the same unrelated filler file and vLLM-vs-itself KL reaches 1.96 nat there; the run reads these rows as highly sensitive to any numerical change and not evidence about the reference.

Q-REPEATHow large is each engine's run-to-run difference on the precision panels (the within-engine noise floor)?

Q2Beyond the teacher's 14K reach (32K-128K), do the engines differ in NLL on long human-written text, and how much do their distributions disagree?
NLL, CuteAFD v3.0.1 − vLLM fork v0.9.2: −0.0005 [−0.0037, +0.0018], not detected; the run states it as no NLL difference larger than about ±0.005 nat at this sample size (6 documents), with per-type intervals not resolved (t-intervals prose [−0.0013, +0.0050], code [−0.0135, +0.0077], audit U3). The engines' distributions disagree by KL 0.014 (JS 0.0028, argmax agreement 96.2%), about 1.8x vLLM's own run-to-run disagreement on matched documents; this does not say which is closer to BF16.

Q-LOADDoes precision change when scoring runs under concurrent serving load?
Not run: CuteAFD's scoring takes an exclusive bench slot, so scoring under concurrent traffic cannot be done 1:1 (declared in the protocol before any run).
protocolnot runq/RESULTS-Q.mdnot run
THINK-OFFDo both stacks honour enable_thinking:false with this checkpoint's chat template?
The template has no thinking-off switch and always ends with <|assistant|><think>; vLLM fork v0.9.2 rendered 147 prompt tokens for both flag values (with false, reasoning arrives in the content), while CuteAFD v3.0.1 renders false as an empty <think></think> (source). The run therefore used thinking on for every T and L request; the audit reads the template and token count as the decisive evidence, the single request as weak.

Per-position KL, NLL and top-1 per arm for P-A (25 windows), P-B (64 windows) and Q2 (18 windows), rounded to 7 significant digits; support data, no reading of its own.

Post hoc, descriptive (no intervals). P-B generated rows, coarse KL p99: V1 0.6095, V1r 0.5859, V2 0.5666, C1 0.6204, C0 0.5818; share of total KL in the worst 1% of positions: V1 0.236, C1 0.212.
post hocdoneq/tails/pb-tails.json, q/tails/pa-tails.json, q/tails/q2-tails.jsonV3-tails-pb, V3-tails-pa
THow fast does each engine serve (per-stream decode, aggregate throughput, time to first token, prefill rate) across input length, content and concurrency?
CuteAFD v3.0.1 / vLLM fork v0.9.2 (no intervals): single-stream decode 1.02-1.41x (largest on reasoning and code prompts); aggregate level at 1K-8K except 1K x 8 streams at 0.87-0.93x, and +6-17% at 32K-128K, which the run reads as mostly faster long prefill (4,400-4,500 vs 3,700-3,900 tok/s); TTFT on 1K prompts 0.41 vs 0.25 s. Per-cell noise is about ±4-8% (a later CuteAFD re-run, CAVEATS) to ±13% (drift), so ratios inside that are not differences; 'code' and 'json' cells measure reasoning text (M2), and inputs are not byte-identical across engines (I2).
protocoldonet/T-SUMMARY.md, t/CAVEATS.md, SUMMARY.mdV3-t-decode-single, V3-t-decode-longctx, V3-t-aggregate, V3-t-ttft
T-CACHEDHow fast is a repeated identical 8K prompt served (prefix-cache reuse)?
CuteAFD v3.0.1 restored the repeated 8K prompt from its prefix cache with TTFT 0.017 s (8,192 cached tokens); vLLM fork v0.9.2 reported cached_tokens 0 and TTFT 2.10 s on every repeat, equal to an uncached 8K prompt (confirmed in audit section 3). Investigated in F2.
protocoldoneREADME.md, AUDIT-2026-10-27.md, f2/RESULTS-F2.mdV3-t-cached
LHow do the engines behave on long outputs staged to 128K: finishing versus cap, loops, task correctness, length and decode rate?
Descriptive, n=2 per prompt. At the 128K cap: code all tests pass 7/8 on both engines (217 vs 212 of 218 tests, vLLM fork v0.9.2 vs CuteAFD v3.0.1), MATH-500 level 5 8/8 on both (ceiling), prose 8/8 stopped on both, JSON valid 6/8 vs 4/8; CuteAFD replies ran ~15-25% shorter (median prose 39K vs 49K, code 38K vs 51K tokens), cause not separated from sampling. Code 'terminal loop' flags (vLLM 6 at L3 and 1 at L3s, CuteAFD 2 at L2 and 4 at L3) have no exact period; the audit (U5) read vLLM's as the answer restating code drafted in the reasoning; at the L1/L2 caps and for the 1,030-record JSON task the cells measure stop-versus-cap, not task completion.

F1Which of CuteAFD's switchable FP8 choices (KDA projections, output head, W8A8 prefill) account for its P-B gap to vLLM at 8-16K?

The run reads W8A8 prefill as accounting for most of the 8-16K difference in this prefill-shaped measurement; decode-path precision was not scored.

F2Why did vLLM v0.9.2 report 0 cached tokens on a repeated 8K prompt with prefix caching on?
vLLM fork v0.9.2: cached tokens 0 on all repeats at 1,024-8,704 tokens; at 17,408 tokens 4,352, 8,704, 8,704 with TTFT 3.457 / 2.313 / 2.309 s. DRIFTDid either engine's serving speed drift between its Block T run and a later repeat of the same requests?
Later / Block T ratio: CuteAFD v3.0.1 decode 0.981 (0.958-1.065), aggregate 0.990 (0.971-1.029); vLLM fork v0.9.2 decode 1.021 (0.931-1.126), aggregate 1.013 (0.935-1.125). The run reads this as no systematic drift at the median, with single cells varying by up to about ±13%, so Block T ratios within about ±10% are not differences.

source readingdoneREADME.md, SUMMARY.mdV3-obs-launcher
I1Was vLLM's run-to-run variation on the precision panels correctly attributed and sized?
Every vLLM capture ran alone with identical chunking, so the variation is not batching or speculation: generated rows are 100% bit-equal between V1 and V1r in 1-step windows, 46% in 2-step and 0% in 4+ step windows; vLLM's self-divergence is 55% (2-8K) and 65% (8-16K) of the cross-engine KL. Against mean(V1, V1r), 8-16K KL +0.0050 [+0.0031, +0.0071], top-1 −0.87 [−1.20, −0.54]; the 2-8K top-1 +0.39 [+0.03, +0.76] against V1 is +0.14 [−0.29, +0.48] against V1r (not detected).
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, q/RESULTS-Q.mdV3-i1-chunks, V3-i1-selfdiv, V3-i1-baseline, V3-i1-midtop1
I2Did Block T give both engines identical inputs?
No: 0 of 1,008 paired requests share a nonce (salt includes the run label and time), so prompts differ in their first line; seeds still pair. The 128K code prompts are 24 tokens longer on CuteAFD v3.0.1 because the vLLM stack parses literal <think> and </think> in user text as special tokens and CuteAFD keeps them as text, so A2's 'same prompt' does not hold for those cells; the audit reads the throughput impact as negligible.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, t/CAVEATS.mdV3-i2-inputs
U1Is the P-B gap 'located at 8-16K and growing with context', or confounded with content?
Confounded: all 12 block-B (long code reading) windows sit at 8-16K and supply 6,144 of its 7,522 rows. Within block A, C1 − mean(V) KL is 0-2K +0.0029 [−0.0013, +0.0083], 2-8K +0.0017 [−0.0005, +0.0033], 8-16K +0.0028 [−0.0003, +0.0055], difference-in-differences +0.0006 [−0.0030, +0.0044]: growth with context not detected for KL. The 8-16K difference survives leave-one-window-out (+0.0037 to +0.0051) and Bonferroni (KL +0.0044 [+0.0003, +0.0082]); headlining the bucket was post hoc framing.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, q/RESULTS-Q.mdV3-u1-blockB, V3-u1-blockA-trend, V3-u1-robust
U2Does the FP8 mechanism story (projections, head, KDA) cover the engines' arithmetic differences, and is F1a a clean ablation?
Narrowed: the engines also differ in DSA index-key format (CuteAFD BF16 keys, vLLM FP8), attention placement, KDA split path, prefill geometry, BF16 logits in vLLM and W8A8 prefill (source and logs, not measured); F1a also drops KDA output sharding, and F1's reading rule is ambiguous. CuteAFD's predictive entropy is higher (8-16K 0.580 vs V1 0.523, 18 of 18 windows); the audit infers, untested, a scale or bias effect rather than zero-mean rounding.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, q/RESULTS-Q.mdV3-u2-arith, V3-u2-entropy
U3Do Q2's sub-results (per-type NLL, '3x' run-to-run ratio) hold with 6 documents?
Overall C1 − V1 NLL −0.0005, t-interval [−0.0046, +0.0035]; prose +0.0019 [−0.0013, +0.0050], code −0.0029 [−0.0135, +0.0077]: neither sub-claim excludes 0. One window (c2-064k) dominates code; on matched documents the engine disagreement is 1.8x vLLM's floor, not 3x. Q2 can bound an NLL difference at about 0.004-0.005 nat, so only a bounded statement is supported.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, q/RESULTS-Q.mdV3-u3-q2-types
U4Do P-B's trailing context rows show a reference-side error?
All 30 trailing spans are near-copies of one unrelated filler file, so effectively one item; cross-engine KL on rows 20+ into the span is 0.72 / 3.14 / 2.53 nats on three windows and vLLM-vs-itself 1.96 nats on a10. The audit reads this as hypersensitive rows, not evidence of a reference-side error.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, q/RESULTS-Q.mdV3-u4-ctx
U5Are Block L's code 'terminal loop' flags loops?
For vLLM fork v0.9.2 (6 of 8 L3 and 1 of 1 L3s flagged): all repeated 50-grams in the final 4,096-token window first occur in the reasoning, content self-repetition 0.000-0.001, 37-88% of content 50-grams copied from the reasoning, no exact period: the answer restates code drafted in the reasoning, not a loop.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, README.mdV3-u5-vllm-code
M1Is Block T protected against drift and thermal or clock variation?
No drift control (no declared CVC subset; V ran before C, no repeat) and no in-run telemetry. F1-base against C1-r1 gives per-cell ratios 0.96-1.08 on decode and 0.96-1.05 on aggregate, so single-cell noise is about ±4-8% and T-SUMMARY ratios between 0.95 and 1.08 cannot be read as differences; T-SUMMARY has no intervals.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, t/CAVEATS.mdV3-m1-noise
M2Is the single-stream speed difference explained by draft policy, and do the content labels describe the output?
Draft policies differ (vLLM 3 fixed BF16 draft tokens; CuteAFD adaptive with an FP8 drafter and W8A8 draft linear) and acceptance was not recorded; the audit reads the 1.16-1.41x on code, json and reasoning vs 1.02-1.06 on prose as consistent with deeper drafting on predictable text. At 1,024 output tokens, 0% of code and json requests on either engine left the reasoning.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, t/CAVEATS.mdV3-m2-labels, V3-m2-draft
M3Which launch settings differ between the engines beyond the documented deltas?
Undocumented on vLLM: GPU_MEMORY_UTILIZATION 0.95 (launcher default 0.97) and the v0.9.0 repo's start.sh run with the v0.9.2 image (v0.9.2 launcher defaults unverified). Further differences: drafter policy, attention placement (split:25 vs context), DSA index K (FP8 kpool vs BF16 keys), KV record, prefill scheduling, prefix cache, logits (BF16 vs F32), special tokens in user text.
checkdoneAUDIT-2026-10-27.md, t/CAVEATS.mdV3-m3-launch
M4Is CuteAFD within the P-B panel's own paired bar against vLLM?
Upper 95% bound of CuteAFD's loss: top-1 0.0080 (vs V1) / 0.0067 (vs V1r), KL 0.0043 / 0.0048 against a 0.005 bar: not shown within the bar on top-1; within it on KL, narrowly. RESULTS-Q.md notes the bar is defined for same-engine changes and is applied here by analogy only.

M5Where does the P-B panel's generated text come from?
P-B's generated rows are greedy (temperature 0) outputs of CuteAFD v1.0.0 serving official FP8 with a BF16 head, kda-fp8 off, fp8-prefill none; any engine-family bias would favour CuteAFD, against the observed difference; on greedy text NLL measures sharpness.

M6Was decode-shaped precision scored?
No: only prefill-shaped scoring was run, although tpurtell's CLI has --score-path decode. Precision of the path generated tokens use was not measured in this run.

2026-10-26 · CuteAFD router logits across its 160/161-row route switch (one GPU, one kernel)

Are CuteAFD's GLM-5.3-Flash router logits bit-identical across the switch between its skinny GEMV route (up to 160 rows) and its TMA tensor-core route, a question its source audit left open?

exploratory · protocol: none (not a frozen-protocol run) · data: results/2026-10-26-e1-router-route

Findings written to the current standard (4)
descriptive Are CuteAFD's router logits bit-identical across its skinny-GEMV (up to 160 rows) / TMA tensor-core (above 160 rows) route switch?

Exploratory, one GPU, one kernel, approximate inputs: the same rows launched at 160 (skinny) and 161 (TMA) rows give bit-identical logits for 0.35% to 0.37% of logits per cell, with a largest absolute logit difference per cell of 1.7e-5 to 8.2e-5 (64,000 rows over 10 cells).

descriptive What capacity does each engine report at startup with K4 on two RTX GPUs, and what does CuteAFD's planner need to admit K4?

Capacity lines at startup: vLLM fork v0.9.2 1,377,179 tokens, 16 sequences; CuteAFD v3.0.1 447,488 tokens, 16 requests (serving launch; its planner's dry run predicted 605,696) and 451,584 on the scoring launch. accounting Before the freeze: do both engines tokenize the same inputs identically, return reasoning separately, and repeat text under a repeated seed?

Prompt tokens equal on the chat smoke (26 and 26) and the 1K Block T prompt (1,024 and 1,024); a test prompt with literal <think> tags gave 33 tokens on the vLLM fork and 37 on CuteAFD (29 on both without the tags); both returned reasoning separately. The same seed twice on 3 prompts gave identical text in 1 of 3 on the vLLM fork and 3 of 3 on CuteAFD (cause not examined).

accounting Where does panel P-A come from, and which checkpoint did its publisher produce?

P-A is the 25 windows final-0000 to final-0024 ('qualification windows') of the dataset brandonmusic/GLM-5.3-Flash-ShapleyMCG-K4-Evidence @ 32afb887, with BF16 teacher logits of zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash-BF16 @ a6c167b6; the same account publishes the K4 checkpoint scored here (brandonmusic/GLM-5.3-Flash-tr3-4bpw). The run states that P-A is drawn from the corpus whose fit and selection windows K4's producer published and so can favour K4, while K3.25 was calibrated on other text.

descriptive Post hoc: on window c2-064k, are rows that complete a long-range exact copy more often volatile between the vLLM fork's two launches?

Post hoc, c2-064k: of 129 rows whose next token completes a 12-gram also seen more than 2,048 tokens earlier, 18 differ by more than 1 nat between the vLLM launches (rate 0.140), against 174 of the other rows (rate 0.044). Whether the sparse-attention indexer's selection explains this is stated by the run as an open question, not tested.

descriptive Are each engine's scored rows reproducible, within a launch and across launches, on K4?

CuteAFD v3.0.1's two passes on one scoring launch were identical on every window (row hashes; P-A 25 of 25, P-B 64 of 64, Q2 difference 0). descriptive Did either engine's speed drift during Block T on K4, and what reading band did that give?

Drift subsets, later ÷ earlier median decode per cell: vLLM fork 0.94 to 1.07 at the end of its session and 0.98 to 1.02 at the end of the stage, CuteAFD 0.97 to 1.06; every drift ratio stayed within 8%, so the Block T band is 0.08 for both engines.

descriptive With K4, how do the two engines' long replies end and pass their content checks per cap and content?

vLLM fork / CuteAFD, 8 replies per cell: at the 130,048 cap code all tests pass 8/8 and 8/8, reasoning correct 8/8 and 8/8, JSON valid 5/8 and 3/8, prose finished 7/8 and 8/8; at the 32,768 cap code finished 0/8 and 5/8 (all passing), JSON valid 2/8 and 2/8; at the 8,192 cap only reasoning finished (8/8 each). JSON tasks are sized for thinking off and are infeasible by design where the reasoning fills the cap.

accounting Which of CuteAFD's FP8 switch arms fit a serving or scoring launch with K4 on 2x RTX?

S1-STARTHow long does each engine take from the start command to /v1/models, to ready, and to a first token, cold and warm, with K4?
Descriptive, one cold and one warm start, K4: vLLM fork v0.9.2 ready in 307 s cold and 257 s warm (/v1/models 249 s and 200 s); CuteAFD v3.0.1 76 s cold and 86 s warm (/v1/models 74 s and 79 s); CuteAFD scoring launch 90 s.
protocoldonestartup/startup.json, README.mdK4-startup-time
S1-POOLWhat capacity does each engine report at startup with K4, and what local settings does the planner need to admit K4 on two RTX GPUs?
vLLM fork v0.9.2: KV pool 1,377,179 tokens, 16 sequences. CuteAFD v3.0.1: 447,488 tokens, 16 requests on the serving launch (the dry run predicted 605,696; the run attributes the rest to the desktop's context on GPU 1), 451,584 on the scoring launch. At its defaults CuteAFD's planner refuses K4 on two RTX GPUs (pool 260,608 tokens below the 1,048,576-token context floor); the run sets MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS=262144.
protocoldonestartup/startup.json, METHOD.mdK4-startup-capacity
S1-SMOKEDo both engines tokenize the same inputs identically, return reasoning separately, and behave the same with literal <think> text and a repeated seed?
Prompt tokens equal on the chat smoke (26/26) and the 1K Block T prompt (1,024/1,024); literal <think> tags give 33 tokens on the vLLM fork and 37 on CuteAFD (29 on both without the tags; the run reads this as vLLM parsing them as special tokens). Same seed twice: vLLM identical text 1 of 3, CuteAFD 3 of 3 (cause not examined).

C2On Q2 document c2, where do the vLLM fork's two launches and CuteAFD disagree by more than 1 nat, and what kind of rows are they?
Post hoc, K4: on c2-064k the two vLLM fork launches differ from each other on 192 rows by more than 1 nat (CuteAFD against them: 292 and 298); c2-032k 9, c2-128k 70, controls 0-9. Of the 192, 38 have one launch above 0.9 probability on the actual token and 20 are top-1/top-2 swaps. Between the vLLM launches on c2-064k, rows completing a 12-gram also seen more than 2,048 tokens earlier are volatile at 14% against 4.4% for other rows. Whether near-duplicate code and the sparse-attention indexer's selection explain the flips is stated as an open question, not tested.

DETAre each engine's scored rows reproducible within a launch and across launches?
CuteAFD v3.0.1's two passes were byte-identical on every window (row hashes). The vLLM fork v0.9.2's two launches returned identical values on 20 of 64 P-B windows and none of the 25 P-A windows (mean P-A KL20 differs by less than 1e-7); its within-launch P-B repeat matched launch 1 on 20 of 64 windows. The method records vLLM's multi-chunk prefill as not run-to-run deterministic past the first 2,045-token chunk.

2026-10-27 · Needle retrieval at 256K, 512K and 1M tokens, both engines, K3.25 (one request per prompt and engine)

Do tpurtell's vLLM fork v0.9.2 and CuteAFD v3.0.1, serving K3.25, accept prompts of 256K, 512K and 1M tokens and return planted needles from them?

exploratory · protocol: none (not a frozen-protocol run) · data: results/2026-10-27-long-context-pilot

descriptive At 262,144, 524,288 and 1,000,000 content tokens, do the vLLM fork v0.9.2 (K3.25-v1) and CuteAFD v3.0.1 (K3.25-Core-FP8) finish on their own and return a planted code, and six planted records in order?

PHASESWhere does the vLLM fork's startup time go, phase by phase, with and without persisted caches, and how large are the persisted caches?

Persisted, warm: 254 s, 0 compiles, 58 s graph-capture phase, ~64 s warm-up. Persisted cache sizes: TileLang 11 MB, Triton 88 MB, TorchInductor 6.1 MB. 2026-11-02 · Thinking length and the 6-bit core of K4-Core-K6: BF16 core-tensor swaps on fixed traces (G2) and in free generation (G4), with loop checks

Does storing K4-Core-K6's 316 core tensors in 6 bits instead of BF16 make the model think longer, on the vLLM fork with a local loader modification?

measurement · protocol: protocol/PLAN-EXCERPT.md · data: results/2026-11-02-thinking-length

Findings written to the current standard (22)
accounting Before any GPU stage: is the BF16 core file correct, does the loader swap switch load exactly the chosen groups, and which probe traces were built?

All 316 BF16 core tensors are within 6-bit error of K4-Core-K6's decoded tensors (relative error 0.0190 to 0.0246, median 0.0197; pass at 0.05) and the loader swap test passes for 12 owners. G0 built 84 probe traces (60 finished, 24 not) and G2 scored 52 of that kind (40 finished, 12 not); no published document states how the 52 were selected.

difference On the vLLM fork's G2 dumps, does any core group's top-64 KL to the 6-bit core grow with position along the fixed science-question traces?

Exploratory (criteria written before computing): the per-trace slope of top-64 KL on log2(position) is below 0 for every swap arm except lm_head, e.g. whole core −0.019 [−0.055, −0.0008], mla_q_b −0.020 [−0.054, −0.0006], mla_o −0.011 [−0.030, −0.0007]. Exploratory. difference On CuteAFD v4.0.0 with the BF16-core checkpoint, does switching the KDA launch setting off (GLM5_FLASH_KDA_FP8=off: KDA projections from row128 FP8 to BF16 and, on this two-GPU split, KDA output sharding off) change code reasoning length?

F-kda / C-K6x reasoning tokens 1.31 [1.24, 1.38] on the 12 new prompts (48 seed pairs, 12 of 12 prompts above 1); above 1 = the arm with the KDA setting off thought longer. The setting changes both the KDA projection format and KDA output sharding, and the run does not separate the two.

source reading What are the switched-off settings in CuteAFD v4.0.0's design, and what do its documents say about them?

At v4.0.0 the switched-off settings are the launcher's defaults: row128 FP8 KDA and an FP8 head on both layouts and, on the two-GPU head split, token-row KDA output ownership; the model notes and the --kda-fp8 option text describe BF16 KDA as the two-GPU default, while the launcher selects row128, which every launch here logged. The checks cited in those documents are teacher-forced on 512 positions; this run measures free generation of long reasoning.

  • results/2026-11-06-cuteafd-fp8-ablation/FA-RESULT.md — design context (added 2026-11-08)
source reading Where in CuteAFD v4.0.0 does the code-length difference that moves with the KDA setting arise: prefill or generation?

Not measured; a reading of the source and of this run's sizes: prompts are 1,155-1,758 tokens against median reasoning of 50-68K tokens, speculative verify steps run on the decode programs, which read the FP8 KDA copies, KDA output sharding is not limited to prefill, and the KDA setting also changes the KDA projections in prefill, whose recurrent state seeds every later token. Prefill against generation, plain decode against speculative verify, and KDA format against output sharding are not separated (the run's document judges the generation path more likely).

DESIGN-CONTEXTWhat are the switched-off settings in CuteAFD v4.0.0's design, and what reasons does the engine's author give for them?
The settings are the launcher's defaults: row128 FP8 KDA and FP8 head on both layouts; on the two-GPU head split, token-row KDA output ownership. The model notes and the option text describe BF16 KDA as the two-GPU default; the launcher comment and code select row128, which every launch logged. The cited author checks are teacher-forced on 512 positions.
source readingdoneFA-RESULT.mdFA-design-context
WHEREWhere in the engine does the code-length difference arise: prefill or generation?
Judgement, not a measurement: most likely on the generation path (decode and speculative verify); prefill not excluded. Prompts are 1,155-1,758 tokens against medians of 50-68K reasoning tokens. Open: KDA in prefill vs generation; plain decode vs speculative verify; KDA format vs output sharding.

C-K6 vs C-K6x +1.6 [-7.8, +10.9], not detected. F arms ran a day after the C arms; V arms are another engine; one prompt family.

source reading On CuteAFD v4.0.0, what besides the core's weights differs at launch between the native 6-bit core arm and the BF16-core arm?

By the launch logs and the v4.0.0 source: the BF16-core arm (and K4) runs the KDA projections in row128 FP8 (with the f_a, g_a and b projections packed into the FP8 operand), an FP8 vocabulary head and FP8 prefill activations for MLA and FFN, while the native 6-bit arm runs KDA and the head on its 6-bit kernels without activation quantization. MLA, dense and shared-expert projections are FP8 on the BF16 path, with no BF16 option on v4.

source reading On CuteAFD v4.0.0 on two GPUs, does switching KDA FP8 off change anything besides the KDA projections' format?

On the two-GPU head split the launcher's default also adds KDA output sharding (token-row output ownership) unless KDA FP8 is off; the daemon accepts it only with FP8 KDA and clears it for a native 6-bit core. Switching KDA FP8 off on two GPUs therefore also returns KDA to the partials split path.

source reading What reasons does CuteAFD v4.0.0's own documentation give for these launch defaults, and do its documents agree?

The settings are the launcher's defaults, with reasons in its comment: row128 KDA and an FP8 head on both layouts, and on the two-GPU split token-row KDA output ownership to avoid rounding the two GPUs' half-K BF16 partials; the model notes give the speed trade with teacher-forced checks (top-1, KL). The model notes and the --kda-fp8 option text describe BF16 KDA as the two-GPU default, so the documents differ, and the launcher's choice is what runs.

FP8PATHSource reading: what changes on CuteAFD v4.0.0 besides the core's weights when the core is BF16 instead of native 6-bit?
Source and log reading: with the BF16 core the launcher's auto settings put the KDA projections (row128 FP8, including f_a/g_a/b packed into the FP8 operand) and the vocabulary head in FP8, and prefill runs MLA and FFN with FP8 activations; with the native 6-bit core the daemon turns KDA FP8 and the FP8 head off. MLA, dense and shared-expert projections are FP8-only on the BF16 path. Which of these goes with the shorter reasoning is not separated by this reading.
source readingdoneCUTEAFD-FP8-PATH.mdVC-fp8-path
FP8PATH-SHARDFollow-up source reading: does switching KDA FP8 off on two GPUs change anything else?
On a two-GPU head split the launcher's auto also adds token-row KDA output ownership unless KDA FP8 is off (run-family.sh:2366); the daemon accepts it only with --kda-fp8 row128 or channel (mod.rs:1296-1299) and clears it for a native 6-bit core (mod.rs:1716-1722). So GLM5_FLASH_KDA_FP8=off also returns KDA to the partials split path.
